f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)
,
if
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)
and
{\rm supp}(f)\subseteq H
.
We say that
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)
, if

{\rm supp}\left(f\right)\subseteq H
for some functionally compact
set
H\Subset_{{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Since
H\Subset_{{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
,
then
f
is also sharply bounded by the extreme value Thm 6 of
[
10
]
.

Theorem 48
.

Let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)

and
\emptyset\neq H\Subset_{{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Then,

(i)

{\rm ext}(H)
is sharply open, and

(ii)

f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)

if and only if
f\mid_{{\rm ext}(H)}=0
.

(iii)

If
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)

then
f^{(n)}(x)=0
for all
x\in{\rm ext}(H)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In
particular, if
H\subseteq[-h,h]
,
h>0
, then
f^{(n)}(x)=0
whenever

x\in(-\infty,-h]\vee[h,+\infty)
(see
[
10
, Def. 35]
for
the definition of
\vee
).

Proof.

See
[
8
, Lem. 3.3]
and
[
8
, Thm. 3.4]
.
∎

Example 49
.

(i)

Since the
1
-dimensional Dirac delta
\delta_{1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)

and
\delta_{1}(0)>0
, then
\delta_{1}\mid_{B_{s}(0)}>0
for some

s\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
by the sharp continuity of
\delta_{1}
. Hence

B_{s}(0)\subseteq{\rm supp}\left(\delta_{1}\right)\subseteq[-r,r]
,
for all
r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

(ii)

Consider the GSF corresponding to the Gaussian
f(x):=e^{-\frac{|x|^{2}}{2}}
,
for all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. Then
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}\left(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)

and it is not hard to prove that
0\leq f(x)\leq\left|x\right|^{-q}

for all
q\in\mathbb{N}
and for
|x|
finite sufficiently large. Therefore,
for any strongly infinite numbers
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, we have

f(x)=0
for all
x\in{\rm ext}\left(\left[-h,h\right]\right)
.
Hence,
{\rm supp}(f)\subseteq[-h,h]
.

We now define the hyperfinite Fourier transform (HFT). For more details
and proofs about HFT, the reader can refer to
[
8
]
.

Definition 50
.

Let
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
be a positive
infinite number,
H:=[-h,h]\subseteq\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
.
We define
the hyperfinite Fourier transform (HFT)
\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)

of
f
on

H
as follows:

\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(\omega):=\int_{-h}^{h}f(x)e^{-ix\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}},\quad\forall\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}.

(4.1)

The term
hyperfinite
captures a deliberate duality: while

h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
is an infinite number, generalized smooth functions
treat functionally compact sets like
H
as though they were finite
with compact structures. Thus,
hyperfinite
reflects an entity
that is infinite in scale but bounded in several points of view.

Clearly,
\mathcal{F}_{h}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})

and for all
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
and
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

we have

\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(\omega)\right|\leq\int_{H}|f(x)|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}},

(4.2)

so that the HFT is always sharply bounded on
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
. We also
note that if
h=[h_{\varepsilon}]
, then

\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(f\right)\left(\omega\right)=\left[\intop_{-h_{\varepsilon}}^{h_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}\left(x\right)e^{-ix\cdotp\omega_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}\right]=\left[\hat{\mathcal{F}}(\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon})(\omega_{\varepsilon})\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}},

where
\hat{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
is the
classical FT, and
\chi_{H_{\varepsilon}}
is the characteristic function
of
H_{\varepsilon}:=[-h_{\varepsilon},h_{\varepsilon}]
.

We naturally define the inverse HFT as follows:

Definition 51
.

Let
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
be a positive infinite
number,
H:=[-h,h]\Subset_{{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
.
The inverse HFT is

\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(f)(x):=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-h}^{h}f(\omega)e^{ix\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\quad\forall x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}.

Similarly,
\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
.
We should clarify that the notation
\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}
is an
abuse of notation, as the codomain of
\mathcal{F}_{h}
does not
coincide with the domain of
\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}
(and vice-versa).
When dealing with inversion properties, it is therefore preferable
to consider that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}\mid_{H}

\displaystyle:=\left(-\right)\mid_{H}\circ\mathcal{F}_{h}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}\mid_{H}

\displaystyle:=\left(-\right)\mid_{H}\circ\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}:\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})\longrightarrow\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}).

For this HFT, several properties can be extended, see
[
8
]
.
The main differences concern the HFT of a derivative

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(f^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=i\omega\mathcal{F}_{h}\left(f\right)+\Delta_{1h}f

(4.3)

\displaystyle\Delta_{1h}f(\omega):

\displaystyle=\left[f(x)e^{-ix\cdot\omega}\right]_{x=-h}^{x=h}.

and the Fourier inversion theorem

\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)\right)(y)=\lim_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{k}\left(\mathcal{F}_{h}^{-1}(f)\right)(y)=f(y)\quad\forall y\in\mathring{H}.

We also finally recall that the
\Delta_{1h}f
term appearing in
(
4.3
) is essential e.g. to obtain non trivial solutions
of differential equations, such as the exponential one of the simplest
ODE:
y^{\prime}=y
,
y(0)=1
(this example also emphasizes the importance
to consider the HFT
\mathcal{F}_{h}(y)
for any GSF
y
, not necessarily
of tempered type).

The following result represents the Riemann-Lebesgue lemma in our
framework. It immediately highlights an important difference with
respect to the classical approach since it states that the restriction
to
\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
of the HFT of a very large class of compactly supported
GSF is still compactly supported.

Lemma 52

(Riemann-Lebesgue)
.

Let
H=[H_{\varepsilon}]\Subset_{{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

and
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right)
be a compactly supported
GSF. Assume that
f
is uniformly bounded by a tame polynomial, i.e.
4.4

\exists C,b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\,\forall x\in H\,\forall j\in\mathbb{N}:\left|f^{(j)}(x)\right|\leq C\cdot b^{j}.

(4.4)

For all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, if
\omega
is invertible,
then

\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(\omega)\right|\leq\frac{1}{|\omega|^{N}}\cdot\int_{H}\left|f^{(N)}(x)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}.

(4.5)

Therefore,
\lim_{\omega\rightarrow+\infty}\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(\omega)\right|=0

(in the sharp topology). Actually, (
4.5
) yields the
stronger result:

\exists Q\in\mathbb{N}:\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)|_{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}\right).

(4.6)

Proof.

For the first part of the proof, see
[
8
, Lem. 5.1]
. Here,
we only prove (
4.6
). We first recall that
\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}\Subset_{{\rm f}}\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
C
,
b\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
from (
4.4
) and

\lambda(H):=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\rightarrow+\infty\\
m\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\left[\lambda\left(\overline{B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\text{\rm E}}}}_{\rho_{\varepsilon}^{m}}\left(H_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right]\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}},

where
\lambda
is the Lebesgue measure. Therefore,
b\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-R}

for some
R\in\mathbb{N}
, and we can set
Q:=R+1
. We want to prove the
claim using Thm.
48
(ii)
,
so that we take
\omega\in{\rm ext}\left(\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}\right)
.
It cannot be
|\omega|<_{{\rm s}}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}
because this would
yields
\left|\omega-a\right|=_{{\rm s}}0
for some
a\in\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}
;
consequently,
|\omega|\geq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}
. We apply assumption (
4.4
)
and inequality (
4.5
) with an arbitrary
N\in\mathbb{N}

to get

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(\omega)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\omega|^{N}}\cdot\int_{H}\left|f^{(N)}(x)\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\hbox{\rm d}x}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}x$}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{NQ}Cb^{N}\lambda(H)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{N(Q-R)}\lambda(H)=C\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{N}\lambda(H).

For
N\rightarrow+\infty
, we hence have
\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(\omega)=0

if
\omega\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
.
∎

We close this section with a Paley-Wiener type theorem. We again underscore
that this result states that the HFT
\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)
of
any

GSF
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
is always an entire GHF without assuming
that
f
is compactly supported. As it will be clear from the proof,
this is a consequence of the functionally compact integration domain

H
, even if
H\supseteq\mathbb{R}
. In the end, it is a consequence of
having a good non-Archimedean setting with infinite numbers.

Theorem 53

(Paley-Wiener)
.

Let
h\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, set
H:=[-h,h]
,
and let
f\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GC}^{\infty}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
. Then the HFT
\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)

satisfies the following properties:

(i)

\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{G}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GH}(\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}},\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})

is a GHF.

(ii)

If
h
is of logarithmic type, i.e.
h\leq Q\log\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}
,
for some
Q\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, then
\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)
is an entire GHF.

(iii)

It is integrabile on the real axis:
\int_{H}\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)\mid_{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}}(\omega)\right|^{2}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}=2\pi\int_{H}\left|f\right|^{2}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}

(Plancherel).

(iv)

It is of exponential type: There exists a constant

C\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
and for all
z\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
:

\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(z)\right|\leq Ce^{h|z|}.

Proof.

Set
\gamma_{s}
be the circle of radius
s\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
and
centered at
z\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}}
. By Fubini theorem, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\gamma_{r}}\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(z)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\gamma_{r}}\left(\int_{H}f(\omega)e^{-iz\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}

\displaystyle=\int_{H}f(\omega)\left(\int_{\gamma_{r}}e^{-iz\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\right)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\hbox{\rm d}z}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}z$}}.

Since
e^{-iz\omega}
is a generalized entire function of
z
, the
inner integral
\int_{\gamma_{r}}e^{-iz\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}=0
by
Cauchy-Goursat’s theorem (see
[
10
, Thm. 45]
). Therefore,
the whole expression is
0
, and by Morera’s theorem (see
[
10
, Thm. 41]
),

\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)
is a GHF. Now, using the Taylor hyperseries
of
\omega\mapsto e^{-iz\omega}
, see Example
35
,
since
h
is of logarithmic type and
\omega\in H
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(\omega)

\displaystyle=\int_{H}f(\omega)\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{(-iz\omega)^{n}}{n!}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{$\hskip-5.49028pt$}$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}{{\vphantom{\sum}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hskip-2.04861pt$}}}\sum_{n\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{N}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{N}}\,}}\frac{(-iz)^{n}}{n!}\int_{H}f(\omega)\omega^{n}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}},

where we use
[
6
, Thm. 75]
to exchange hyperseries
and integration over the functionally compact set
H
. This proves
that
\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)
is an entire function.

By Plancherel’s identity (see
[
8
, Thm. 7.11]
), we have:

\displaystyle\int_{H}\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)\mid_{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}}(\omega)\right|^{2}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{H}\left|f\right|^{2}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}.

Moreover, let
z=x+iy
, then

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(z)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{H}f(\omega)e^{-iz\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{H}\left|f(\omega)\right|\left|e^{-iz\omega}\right|\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}

\displaystyle=\int_{H}\left|f(\omega)\right|e^{y\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}.

We then apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, which in our setting
relies upon the monotonicity of the integral, Fubini’s theorem, and
Young’s inequality. Since the classical proofs of these underlying
properties can be directly adapted to our framework, the inequality
holds as expected (see
[
8
, Thm. 3.10]
). We have:

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}_{h}(f)(z)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{H}\left|f(\omega)\right|e^{y\omega}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{H}\left|f(\omega)\right|^{2}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\left(\int_{H}e^{2h|y|}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{h|z|}\left(\int_{H}\left|f(\omega)\right|^{2}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\left(\int_{H}\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\hbox{\rm d}\omega}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\omega$}}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle=Ce^{h|z|}.

∎

For example, consider the
1
-dimensional Dirac delta
\delta_{1}(x):=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}\mu(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}x)

where
\mu:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\beta)
is an entire mollifier defined
as the inverse Fourier transform of any smooth compactly supported
function
\beta\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R})
with
\beta(0)=1
(see
[
9
, Sec. 5]

for more details). We have
\delta_{1}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}(H,\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{C}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{C}})
.
For all
x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, we have
\delta_{1}^{(j)}(x)=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-j-1}\mu^{(j)}(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-1}x)

and

\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{(j)}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-j-1}\left[\frac{{\rm d}^{j}}{{\rm d}x^{j}}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\beta(t)e^{i\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}xt}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-j-1}}{2\pi}\left[\int\left(i\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}t\right)^{j}\beta(t)e^{i\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}xt}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}\right]

\displaystyle\left|\delta_{1}^{(j)}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-2j-1}}{2\pi}\int_{-1}^{1}|t|^{j}\beta(t)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\hbox{\rm d}t}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}t$}}=:C\left(\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-2}\right)^{j}.

Thus, the
1
-dimensional Dirac’s delta satisfies condition (
4.4
),
i.e. it is uniformly bounded by a tame polynomial. By Riemann-Lebesgue
Lem.
52
, we have

\exists Q\in\mathbb{N}:f=\mathcal{F}_{h}(\delta_{1})|_{\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}}\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathcal{D}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\mathcal{GD}\left(\overline{B_{\mathchoice{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\hbox{\rm d}\rho}{\scalebox{0.75}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}{\scalebox{0.35}{$\hbox{\rm d}\rho$}}^{-Q}}(0)}\right).

On the other hand, the Paley-Wiener Thm.
53
,
yields that
\mathds{1}:=\mathcal{F}_{h}(\delta_{1})
is an entire
GHF if
h
is of logarithmic type, hence it is surely
not

compactly supported because of Liouville Thm.
43
.
It is possible to prove that
\mathds{1}(x)=1
for all
finite

x\in\mathchoice{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.56}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.47}{$\rho$}}}{{\vphantom{\mathbb{R}}}{}^{\scalebox{0.35}{$\rho$}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{R}}
, see
[
8
, Rem. 5.2]
. Moreover, it is also
possible to prove that if
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
and
T\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
,
then
\mathcal{F}(f)=\mathcal{F}(f\cdot\mathds{1})=\iota(\hat{f})

and
\mathcal{F}_{h}(\iota(T))=\iota(\hat{T})
, where
\hat{(-)}

is the classical FT and
\iota
is the embedding of distributions
(see
[
9
]
).

The converse of Thm.
53
remains an open problem.

5.
Comparison with Colombeau theory and conclusions

The analyticity of Colombeau holomorphic generalized functions (see
e.g.
[
2
,
1
,
4
,
3
,
11
,
15
,
12
]
and
references therein) has been studied at different level of generality.
For instance,
[
4
,
3
]
note that because
holomorphic generalized functions are defined on
\mathbb{C}
, classical
Taylor series do not allow one to obtain several desired results.
Specifically,
[
4
, Sec. 7]
provides three examples
of holomorphic generalized functions that fail to satisfy the principle
of analytic continuation.

Conversely, a version of Goursat’s theorem was proven in
[
1
]

as a consequence of the theory of path integration for generalized
functions on generalized subsets, known in
[
1
]
as
membranes (notably, a membrane is an internal set). This work was
preceded by the theory of discontinuous Colombeau differential calculus

[
2
]
, which allows for the arbitrary composition of
suitable classes of generalized functions. However, in
[
1
]
,
path integral are considered only for the composition
f(\gamma_{t})

with a Colombeau generalized function
f\in\mathcal{G}(\Omega)
,

\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{C}
. This composition is defined only if the path
is generated by a history
(\gamma_{\varepsilon})
, so that
(\gamma_{\varepsilon}([0,1]))

is a pre-membrane, a condition similar to c-boundedness (see
[
1
, Def. 3.1, 3.2]
).
Consequently, Goursat’s theorem holds only on the set of compactly
supported points, which, we recall, can be defined as:

\left\langle\Omega\right\rangle_{{\rm fin}}:=\left\{z\in\left\langle\Omega\right\rangle\mid z\text{ is finite},\exists r\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}:d\left(z,\partial\Omega\right)\geq r\right\}

(see
[
2
, Thm. 5.2]
).

Subsequently, Vernaeve
[
15
]
used an approach equivalent
to GSF theory, but using quotients instead of directly treating generalized
functions as ordinary maps. As established in
[
15
, Prop. 4.18]

(recall also Sec.
1
), while each complex analytic
Colombeau generalized function can be expressed as a Taylor series,
this expansion is valid only within an infinitesimal neighborhood
of each point.

In general, we can conclude this article by considering that in the
study of PDE, the class of analytic functions is sometimes described
as a too rigid set of solutions. In spite of their good properties
with respect to algebraic operations, composition, differentiation,
integration, inversion, etc., this rigidity is essentially well represented
by the identity principle that necessarily excludes e.g. solitons
with compact support or interesting generalized functions. Thanks
to the identity principle
45
, we can state that this
rigidity stems from two main factors: the exclusion of non-Archimedean
numbers from classical analysis and the non-symmetry of analytic continuation
for GHF. The introduction of hyperseries allows one to recover all
these features including also interesting non trivial generalized
functions and compactly supported functions. This development paves
the way for an interesting generalization of the Cauchy-Kowalevski
theorem for GHF that we intend to study in the next work of this series.
